Let's derive a practical optimization algorithm.

Formulation:
Minimize F(p)=max_{k=0}^{2n-2} q_k, with p_i = a_i / S, sum p_i=1, p_i≥0. Score =2nF. Since a can be arbitrary scale, we can return p (sum=1) or amplified. Clipping max to 1000; if p_i ~1/n ≤1, fine. Sum=1 not too small.

Need optimize F. We can choose n. Larger n may allow lower F. But gradient with FFT O(nlogn); for n=50000, one conv ~ maybe 0.05s? In Python with scipy? Could do thousands iterations? Need not too many.

Local optimization options:

A. Multiplicative gradient on smooth max:
F_τ(p) = τ log ∑ exp(q_k/τ) (or max-smooth). q = conv(p,p). Gradient wrt p_i: ∑_k (∂F/∂q_k) p_{k-i}. If we use logsumexp, weights s_k = softmax(q/τ); grad = (conv? s * p) at certain lags. We can compute grad_i = ∑_k s_k p_{k-i}. This is circular? For each i, indices k-i in [0,n-1]. This is like correlation between s and p. We can compute via FFT: conv(s, p) length 3n-1? But s length 2n-1, p length n. Need for each i (0..n-1), sum over k such that j=k-i in [0,n-1] s_k p_j = convolution of s and reversed? Define g_i = ∑_{j=0}^{n-1} s_{i+j} p_j (since k=i+j). So g = (s * p) where s is length 2n-1 and p, but we need first n coefficients of convolution s? Actually (s ⋅ p) with sum s_{i+j} p_j. This is cross-correlation of s and p at lag -i. Can compute by FFT: convolve p with s? p*j? Let r = convolution(p, s) length 3n-2: r_m = ∑_j p_j s_{m-j}. We need r_i = ∑_j p_j s_{i-j}? Not. We need ∑_j p_j s_{i+j}. This is anti-convolution. If define s_rev[j] = s_{L-1-j}? Or use FFT: For g_i=∑_{j=0}^{n-1} p_j s_{i+j}. This is coefficient of? Let vector v on indices 0..2n-2 s. Cross-correlation (p ⊛ s)_i = ∑ p_j s_{i+j}. Yes, can compute via FFT by zero padding and multiplying P(conj?)? Cross-correlation in 1D numpy.correlate: np.correlate(s, p, mode='valid')[i] = ∑_j s_{i+j} p_j if s length ≥ n, p length n. Indeed np.correlate(s, p, mode='valid') returns length len(s)-len(p)+1 = n. So g = np.correlate(s, p, 'valid') (if s as first arg? np.correlate(a,v) = ∑ a[n+k] conj(v[k])? B. Update p. Multiplicative gradient: p ← p * exp(-η g_i) / sum. But g_i are ~? q_i average ~1/(2n). s_i=softmax(q/τ). If τ small, s concentrated on peak, g_i is p at positions contributing to peak. Update reduces those p. Then normalization may increase others. Should reduce peak but may create new peaks.

Can also add entropy to keep from zero: p_i = p_i * exp(-η g_i + ε p_i?) or add small floor and reclip. Use p = p + floor; p normalized. Or use exponent with smoothing: p_new ∝ p * exp(-η g) but p may underflow if many iterations. Use log p representation: ℓ_i. Update ℓ_i ← ℓ_i - η g_i, then subtract max for normalization? p = exp(ℓ - logsumexp). That handles. Initial ℓ all 0 (uniform). But if g_i large uniform, it cancels. ℓ can drift; subtract max each iteration. This is projected multiplicative. It may converge to distribution avoiding peaks. Need choose η. Score uses p normalized; multiplicative update is like natural gradient on simplex. Could be effective.

Could also use gradient descent on F_τ: ℓ_i += -η * (grad_i - p_i * average?). Because normalization simplex: gradient on log p with softmax: for F(p), steepest descent on ℓ: ℓ_i -= η (∂F/∂p_i - p_i ∑p_j∂F/∂p_j?) The natural exponential family update: p_i <- p_i exp(-η (grad_i - E_p[grad])) to keep sum. But multiplicative normalization automatically accounts. Could use centering to improve.

Need compute q and grad. q = convolution(p,p) length 2n-1. max q. F_score = 2n max q (since sum 1). Actually objective with p: sequence p, sum=1, max_b=max q, score=2n max q. Good.

Potential issues: p becomes multimodal, many small atoms, continuous density with oscillations. This is fine. But objective discrete can have large boundaries? We can check.

C. Simulated annealing / coordinate updates: Pick active peak k; for each i contributing, p_i, p_{k-i} product. To reduce q_k, we can move mass between index pairs? Coordinate descent: Choose k peak, find i where p_i p_{k-i} largest, reduce one and increase other? But need smooth.

D. Use **Alternating Direction Method of Multipliers (ADMM)** / primal dual for constraints q_k≤m. For fixed m, minimize 0 subject to p in simplex, q_k≤m. Nonconvex. But can do penalty method: minimize (1/2)∑(q_k - m)_+^2. Gradient similar. Waterfilling.

E. Use **Iterative proportional fitting / Sinkhorn** on sum matrix: We want a matrix X_{ij} = p_i p_j with row/col sums p and diagonal sums q_k≤m. If q target uniform m, X is rank-1 outer product. We can find p such that row sums of matrix U with constraints? Maybe use matrix scaling? Not.

F. Use **ADMM with variable X?** Lift: minimize ||X - a a^T|| subject diag/sups? Probably not.

Let's design custom fast convolution:
```
def next_power_of_two(m):
    if m<=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def fast_full_conv(a):
    n = len(a)
    N=next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]
    # Clip tiny negatives
    out[out<0]=0
    return out
```
But `irfft` of square gives real full length. For large N, good. If a sum not 1, q scales.

For guide:
```
def fast_guide(s, p):
    # s length L=2n-1? But s may length 2n-1.
    n=len(p); L=len(s)
    # need g_i = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} s[i+j] p[j]
    # conv s with p_rev: length L+n-1
    N=next_power_of_two(L+n-1)
    fs=np.fft.rfft(s,N)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1],N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fs*fp,N)
    g=c[n-1:2*n-1]
    g[g<0]=0
    return g
```
Need ensure alignment. Let's verify with small example manually. s=[s0,s1,s2], p=[p0,p1,p2] n=3. p_rev=[p2,p1,p0]. conv c length 5:
c0=s0 p2
c1=s0 p1+s1 p2
c2=s0 p0+s1 p1+s2 p2
c3=s1 p0+s2 p1
c4=s2 p0
We need g_i = ∑_{j=0}^2 s_{i+j} p_j:
g0=s0 p0 + s1 p1 + s2 p2 = c2
g1=s1 p0 + s2 p1 = c3
g2=s2 p0 = c4. So slice c[n-1:2n-1]=c[2:5]. Good. For general, yes.

```
def random_smooth_potential(n, rng, kappa, n_freq=16):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
   phi=np.zeros(n)
   for m in range(1,n_freq+1):
      amp = 1.0/np.sqrt(m)
      if rng.random()<0.7: amp*=rng.uniform(0.2,1.0)
      phase = rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)
      phi += amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x + phase)
   phi -= phi.mean()
   p=np.exp(kappa*phi)
   p += 1e-3
   p=np.clip(p,0,1000); p/=p.sum(); return p
```
Could also include linear terms via m=1 sinus; fine.

```
def eval_seq_array(arr):
   try:
      seq = [float(x) for x in np.clip(arr,0,1000).tolist()]
      val = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
      if np.isinf(val): val = np.inf
      return val
   except Exception:
      return np.inf
```

Optimization function in detail:
```
def optimize_sequence(n, initial_p, budget_s, seed, target_margin=0.0, print_every=1000):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + budget_s
    p = _normalize_p(initial_p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = _custom_score_p(best_p)
    # current p maybe set to best
    p = best_p.copy()
    it = 0
    last_eval_time = time.monotonic()
    exact_score = np.inf
    # Maybe exact initial? Not needed.
    total_iters_target? We loop while time.
    base_eta=0.4, end_eta=2.0
    tau0=0.8, tau_end=0.02
    mix0=0.02, mix_end=0.002
    no_improve=0
    # To avoid infinite if q max=0 (shouldn't)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it +=1
        frac = min(1.0, elapsed / max(budget_s,1e-6))? Need if budget may be 0.
        tau_frac = max(tau_end, tau0 * (1 - frac)**? or exp(-3*frac))
        eta = base_eta + (end_eta - base_eta)*frac
        mix = mix0 + (mix_end-mix0)*frac
        q = _fast_convolve_self(p)
        maxq = float(q.max())
        score = 2.0 * n * maxq
        if not np.isfinite(score): break
        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = score; best_p = p.copy(); no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve +=1
        # If no improvement many, perturb
        if no_improve > 1000 or (it>500 and no_improve>500):
            # perturb current with noise and maybe reset best?
            noise = _random_smooth_potential(n, rng, kappa=0.3, n_freq=8)
            p = _normalize_p((1.0-0.2*p.sum()?) hmm)
            p = _normalize_p(0.8*p + 0.2*noise)
            # Also if stuck at uniform? initial noise.
            no_improve=0
        # weights
        if maxq <= 0: break
        tau = max(maxq * tau_frac, 1e-6 / n?) maybe absolute.
        # Use q - min? For logsumexp, z=(q-max)/tau. If tau small and min far, clip.
        z = (q - maxq) / tau
        z[z < -40] = -40 (to prevent underflow but not needed; exp(-100)=3e-44 fine but could underflow to0; 40 ok)
        s = np.exp(z)
        sm = s.sum()
        if not finite or sm==0: s = q/q.sum()
        else: s /= sm
        guide = _fast_guide(s, p)
        # Clean
        guide = np.nan_to_num(guide, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        mean_guide = float(np.dot(p, guide))
        delta = guide - mean_guide
        max_delta = float(np.max(delta))
        if max_delta <= 1e-300:
            # small random move
            p = _normalize_p(p * (1 + 0.01*rng.standard_normal(n)))
            no_improve=0
            continue
        # Step: p_new = p * exp(-eta*delta/max_delta)
        # If eta large and delta/min ratio maybe >40, but delta/max_delta in [-? ,1]
        step = eta * delta / max_delta
        step = np.clip(step, -50.0, 50.0)
        p_new = p * np.exp(-step)
        p_new = np.clip(p_new, 0.0, None)
        p_new = _normalize_p(p_new, mix=mix)
        # Optionally if score increases too much, revert current? We'll just set p=p_new; if gets worse, best remains.
        p = p_new
        # exact occasional:
        if time.monotonic() - last_eval_time > 10.0 and (score < current_exact? or it%...): exact...
    return best_p, best_score
```

```
def _generate_inits(n, seed, num=8, use_parent=None):
   rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   inits=[]
   # parent upsample if available and parent.n>0
   if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
      pup = _upsample(parent, n); inits.append(pup)
   # scan top shapes? passed.
   for i in range(num):
      kappa = [0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0][i %5]
      p = _random_smooth_potential(n, rng, kappa=kappa, n_freq=max(4, min(32, n//32)))
      inits.append(p)
   # asymmetric linear-quadratic
   for a in [-2,2,-3,3]:
      x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
      p=np.exp(a*x) + 1e-3; normalize; inits.append
   # sine/cos combinations maybe:
   for m in [1,2,3]: for A in [0.5,0.8]: for phase in [0, np.pi/2, np.pi]: p=(1+A*np.sin(2πm*x+phase)); p=max(p,0); normalize;
   return inits
```

Need `upsample` function:
```
def _upsample_to(p, n):
   m=len(p)
   if m==n: return normalize(p)
   if m < 2: return ones
   xs=(np.arange(m)+0.5)/m
   xg=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
   # linear interpolation of probabilities; if p has zeros, okay.
   val=np.interp(xg, xs, p)
   val=np.clip(val,0,1000); val/=val.sum(); return val
```
If p has high freq, interpolation smooths. Could also `val += 1e-3` floor.

- It tries a multiplier list dynamically: e.g., `step_scale` current. On accept, `step_scale = min(max_step, step_scale*1.02)`. On reject, `step_scale *= 0.9`. If step_scale too small after many rejects, revert to best and perturb.
- For weighting, use `tau_frac` decreasing over elapsed in the optimizer.
- If `current_score` is much worse than `best_score` (e.g., > best*1.05) due to perturb, it should converge? We only accept improvements, so current is always an improving path from initial/best? Let's write helper `_score_normalized_p_with_q(p)`:
```
def _score_normalized_p_with_q(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = p.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
       q=np.zeros(2*len(p)-1); return q, np.inf
    pn = p/s
    q = _fast_self_conv(pn)
    maxq = float(q.max())
    score = 2.0*len(pn)*maxq
    return q, score
```
For p already normalized, but okay. Need if len p maybe 0.

```
if n < 128: return np.convolve(p,p)
else fft.
```

Specifically `correlate(a,v, valid)` returns `c_{k}=∑_{i=0}^{N-1} a[i+k] conj(v[i])` where N=len(v). That is exactly ∑ s[i+k] p[i] = g_k! Great. So `g = np.correlate(s, p, mode='valid')` for direct. Now, an important optimization: In `_optimize_sequence`, computing `s = exp((q-max)/tau)` each trial; if tau small, s only top. Guide convolution with s. Then candidate; next iteration recompute s from candidate q. Good.

Can we reuse q for candidate? We compute candidate q via `_fast_self_conv`; yes. This replaces the previous current q; so per accepted/rejected we do one q FFT, one guide FFT. But on the same iteration after computing q and score, guide, update, candidate q/ score = another q FFT. So 2 q FFT + 1 guide FFT per trial. Could we compute candidate guide next iteration; no. Could reduce by using current q for score and candidate q next; That prior version did 1 q per step (at start) + guide. But it didn't know if candidate improved until next. However if we accept blindly, no score check. We can combine: At start, we have current q. After update, we need current q for next. If we accept blindly, next loop's q is candidate q. Then only 1 q +1 guide per iter. But if we want best only on improvements, we can track current_score from q before update. This might be faster. We can maybe use blind natural gradient with adaptive step and periodic evaluation to true score. It might converge faster but risk. Could implement a hybrid: Use blind update for many steps, but every T=10 steps evaluate best? Actually current q computed at start each iteration; if we set p=p_new blindly, current_score known from current q (old). We don't know new score until next start, but that's fine; we can update best when at start old p. If we accept blind, loop uses 1 q +1 guide per iteration. Reversion on long no improvement based on scores from starts. This is faster. Let's consider using this.

Blind gradient:
```
p, q, score = initial_score
best = p, score
stall=0
while time:
   # update p using q
   s... guide... step...
   p_new = normalize(p*exp(-eta*...))
   p = p_new
   q, score = fast_self(p)  # now one q per iter plus guide? Actually we need q for next. This is one q. Guide uses old q. So per iter: guide (1 conv) + new q (1 conv) =2 convs. The previous blind-at-start also 2? Start q (1) + guide (1) each iter =2. Same. The explicit candidate added no extra if we compute candidate q as next start; we were counting old q from previous candidate. Let's map:

Blind start: at top compute q (1); guide (1); update; loop. 2 per trial. Same.
So no difference. Good.
```

Use blind with adaptive step/reversion: At top, we have q from previous update. First q initial. After update, compute candidate q? We can compute at next top. Need ensure best updated with candidate q. We can do:
```

   # current_q corresponds to p (after previous update except first? yes if we set current_q each end)

   p_new = update(p)

```
Then at loop exit, current_q for final p already computed. This is 2 conv per iter after first. Good. Let's implement blind with adaptive learning and revert/perturb. This is simpler and allows schedule.

Pseudo:
```
current = normalize(initial)
current_q = fast_self(current)
current_score = score
best = current; best_q=current_q
best_score=current_score
stall=0
it=0
while time<deadline:
  it++
  # update best with current_score
  if current_score < best_score:
     best=current.copy(); best_q=current_q.copy(); best_score=current_score; stall=0; print
  else: stall += 1
  # if stall > limit, revert/perturb from best
  if stall > stall_limit:
      alpha = min(0.5, 0.1 + 0.01*stall)
      noise = random_smooth(n, rng, 0.4, 8)
      current = normalize((1-alpha)*best + alpha*noise)
      current_q = fast_self(current); current_score=score
      if current_score < best_score: update best
      stall=0; step_scale = 0.3
      continue
  # compute tau and weights from current_q
  ... guide
  # adapt eta: if stall small? step_scale=... 
  # update
  delta...
  p_new = current * exp(-eta*delta/maxdelta)
  p_new = normalize(p_new, mix=0.0)
  # Maybe if p_new all zeros or score nan, revert
  current = p_new
  current_q = fast_self(current); current_score=score
  # if current_score is inf or nan: revert
```

```
          if pool[i][0] > worst_val: worst_idx=i; worst_val=pool[i][0]
```

```
def _optimize_sequence(n, initial_p, deadline, seed, tag=""):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    p = _normalize_p(initial_p)
    q = _fast_self_conv(p); score = 2*n*max(q)
    best = p.copy(); best_q = q.copy(); best_score = score
    best_iter = 0
    it = 0
    eta = 0.7
    min_eta=0.05; max_eta=3.0
    base_eta=0.7
    print initial
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it +=1
       # best already? We should compare before update
       if score < best_score - 1e-14:
          best_score=score; best=p.copy(); best_q=q.copy(); best_iter=it; eta=base_eta? maybe eta *=1.05
       # if many iterations without best improvement
       if it - best_iter > 300:
           # perturb from best
           alpha = 0.1 + 0.1*min(1.0, (it-best_iter)/500.0)
           noise = _random_smooth_potential(n, rng, 0.5, 8)
           p = _normalize_p((1-alpha)*best + alpha*noise)
           q = _fast_self_conv(p); score=...
           if score < best_score: update best
           eta = max(0.3, eta*0.5)
           best_iter = it
           continue
       # weights
       maxq = float(q.max())
       if maxq <= 0: break
       tau_frac = max(0.02, 0.7 * math.exp(-0.002 * it))
       tau = maxq * tau_frac
       z = (q - maxq)/tau
       z = np.clip(z, -35.0, 0.0)
       s = np.exp(z); sm=s.sum()
       if sm <= 0 or not finite: s = q / max(float(q.sum()),1e-300)
       else: s /= sm
       cross = _fast_guide(s,p)
       cross = np.nan_to_num(cross,...)
       mean_cross = float(np.dot(p,cross))
       delta = cross - mean_cross
       max_delta = float(np.max(delta))
       if not finite or max_delta <= 1e-300:
           # random small step
           p = _normalize_p(p * (1.0 + 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n)))
           q=...; score=...
           if score < best_score: update
           eta = max(0.3, eta*0.7)
           continue
       step = eta * delta / max_delta
       step = np.clip(step, -40.0, 40.0)
       p_new = p * np.exp(-step)
       p_new = _normalize_p(p_new)
       # If no finite
       if not finite: p_new=best
       prev_score=score
       q = _fast_self_conv(p_new); score=2*n*max(q)
       p = p_new
       if np.isfinite(score):
           if score < prev_score:
               eta = min(max_eta, eta*1.05)
           else:
               eta = max(min_eta, eta*0.85)
       else:
           p=best; q=best_q; score=best_score; eta=max(min_eta,eta*0.5)
    return best, best_score
```

Implement:
```
def _normalize_p(p, mix=0.0):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    if p.size == 0: return p
    p[p < 0.0] = 0.0
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1e300)
    total = float(p.sum())
    n = p.size
    if total <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0/n, dtype=np.float64)
    p /= total
    if mix > 0.0:
        p = (1.0 - mix) * p + (mix / n)
        # normalize again
        total = p.sum(); if total>0 p/=total
    return p
```
For perturb, we can call `_normalize_p(0.95*best +0.05*noise, mix=0.0)`. If best has zeros and noise floor, okay.


```
def _antisymmetric_smooth_candidate(n, rng, cutoff):
    s = rng.choice([-1,1], size=n)
    s = (s - s[::-1]) / 2  # ensures antisym: s[i] = -s[n-1-i]? Let's check: t=s - s[::-1]; for i: t_i=s_i - s_{n-1-i}; for n-1-i: s_{n-1-i} - s_i = -t_i. Normalize? t_i ∈ {-2,0,2}; set sign: t[t>0]=1, t[t<0]=-1, zeros random? This enforces.
    # Low-pass filter via FFT: keep frequencies <= cutoff
    F=np.fft.rfft(s)
    keep = np.zeros_like(F, bool); keep[:cutoff]=True; F[~keep]=0
    u = np.fft.irfft(F, n); u /= max(np.max(np.abs(u)),1e-12)
    # scale amplitude a in [0.5,1]
    a = rng.uniform(0.5,1.0)
    p = 1.0 + a*u
    p = np.clip(p, 1e-3, None); normalize
```
But if u has mean not zero? Antisymmetric mean zero (if n odd center 0). Good. Low-pass ensures smooth. This can produce a_i ∈ [0,2]. It may be highly structured. Include several cutoffs: n//2? x = np.arange(n)

```
for m in 1..M:

if maxAbs>0: u/=maxAbs
p=1+ kappa*u
p = max(p, 1e-3); normalize
```

Direct branch in `_fast_self_conv`:
```
def _fast_self_conv(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 256:
       q=np.convolve(a,a)
       q = np.asarray(q,float)
       q[q<0]=0
       return q
    N=_next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    q=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    q = q[:2*n-1]
    q[q<0]=0
    return q
```
For n=512, FFT maybe faster; threshold 256 okay.

Fast guide direct branch:
```
def _fast_guide(s,p):
    n=p.size
    if n<=256:
       g=np.correlate(s,p,mode='valid')
       g=np.asarray(g,float); g[g<0]=0; return g
    L=s.size
    N=_next_power_of_two(L+n-1)
    fs=np.fft.rfft(s,N)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fs*fp, N)
    g=c[n-1:2*n-1]
    g[g<0]=0
    return g
```

Let's define `_next_power_of_two` robust:
```
def _next_power_of_two(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    m = int(m)
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```

`_random_smooth_potential(n, seed, kappa, n_freq)` (seed not master):
```
rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
phi = np.zeros(n)
for m in range(1,n_freq+1):
    if rng.random() < 0.8:
       amp = 1.0/np.sqrt(float(m))
       amp *= rng.uniform(0.2,1.0)
       phase = rng.uniform(0, two_pi)
       phi += amp * np.sin(two_pi*m*x + phase)
phi -= phi.mean()
p = np.exp(kappa*phi)
p = np.maximum(p, 1e-3)
return _normalize_p(p)
```

```
maxabs = np.max(np.abs(u))
if maxabs>0: u/=maxabs
p = 1.0 + amplitude*u
p = np.maximum(p, 1e-3)

```

```
def _make_initial_candidates(n, seed, parent, num_random, max_candidates):
    cands=[]
    if parent is not None:
       cands.append(_upsample(parent,n))
    cands.append(np.full(n,1.0/n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
    if n <= 1024:
       for a in [-3,-2,-1,1,2,3]: ...
       for m in [1,2,3,4,5,6,8]:
          for A in [0.5,0.8,1.0]:
             for phase in [0, pi/2, pi, 3pi/2]: ...
       for beta in [0.1,0.3,1,3,10]: ...
    else:
       for a in [-2,2]: ...
       for m in [1,2,3,4]:
          for A in [0.8,1.0]:
             for phase in [math.pi/2, 3*math.pi/2]: ...
    # random smooth and antisym always
    for i in range(num_random): ...
    for i in range(4): antisym
    for i in range(4): binary filtered
    if len(cands)>max_candidates: cands=cands[:max_candidates]
    return cands
```

```
def _upsample(parent, n):
    m = parent.size
    if m == n: return _normalize_p(parent)
    if m <=1: return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    xs=(np.arange(m)+0.5)/float(m)
    xg=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
    # if parent has zero values, interp may produce negative? clip
    vals=np.interp(xg, xs, parent)
    # if n much larger and parent high freq, okay.
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 1e-3)
    return _normalize_p(vals)
```

```

def _evaluate_array(arr):
```

Let's detail `_score_array`:
```
def _score_array(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0: return np.inf
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300: return np.inf
    p = a / s
    n = p.size
    q = _fast_self_conv(p)
    maxq = float(q.max()) if q.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(maxq): return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * maxq)
```
Since objective scale invariant, normalizing to sum1.

`_fast_self_conv` with a maybe length n; if n==0 return empty.
```
def _fast_self_conv(a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.zeros(0,float)
    target = 2*n - 1
    N = _next_power_of_two(target)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)
    q = q[:target]
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    return q
```

`_fast_guide`:
```
def _fast_guide(s,p):
    n = p.size
    if n == 0 or s.size < n: 
       # if invalid, fallback try pad? s should length 2n-1.
       return np.zeros(n,float)
    target = s.size + n -1
    N = _next_power_of_two(target)
    fs = np.fft.rfft(s, N)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fs*fp, N)
    g = c[n-1:2*n-1]
    g = np.maximum(g,0.0)
    return g
```
If s length not 2n-1? We'll pass correct. `target` length 3n-2. Good.

Potential: In guide, s nonnegative but may sum1. `rfft(s,N)` if s length large but N target. Good.

Now, `_upsample`: If parent length >? If parent has values >1000? we normalize. Use `_normalize_p`.
```
def _upsample(parent, n):
    arr=np.asarray(parent,float)
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0); arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
    m=arr.size
    if m==0: return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    if m==n: return _normalize_p(arr)
    if m < 2: return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    xs=(np.arange(m)+0.5)/float(m)
    xg=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/float(n)
    vals=np.interp(xg, xs, arr)
    vals=np.maximum(vals, 1e-3)
    return _normalize_p(vals)
```

Implementation of `_make_initial_candidates` (no nested):
```
def _make_initial_candidates(n, seed, parent, num_random, max_candidates):
    cands=[]
    if parent is not None:
        cands.append(_upsample(parent,n))
    cands.append(np.full(n, 1.0/n))
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    if n <= 1200:
       skews = [-3.0,-2.0,-1.5,-1.0,-0.5,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0]
       for a in skews:
          p = np.exp(a*x); cands.append(_normalize_p(p))
       for m in (1,2,3,4,5,6,8):
          for A in (0.5,0.8,1.0):
             for phase in (0.0, 0.5*math.pi, math.pi, 1.5*math.pi):
                p=1.0 + A*np.sin(_two_pi*m*x + phase)
                p=np.maximum(p,1e-15); cands.append(_normalize_p(p))
       for beta in (0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0):
          p = 1.0/(x+beta)
          cands.append(_normalize_p(p))
    else:
       for a in (-2.0,-1.0,1.0,2.0):
          p=np.exp(a*x); cands.append(_normalize_p(p))
       for m in (1,2,3,4):
          for A in (0.8,1.0):
             for phase in (0.5*math.pi,1.5*math.pi):
                p=1.0 + A*np.sin(_two_pi*m*x+phase)
                p=np.maximum(p,1e-15); cands.append(_normalize_p(p))
    # random smooth
    nfreq = int(min(32, max(4, n//256)))
    for i in range(num_random):
       kappa = (0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0,3.0)[i % 5]
       p = _random_smooth_potential(n, seed + 1000*i + 7, kappa, nfreq)
       cands.append(p)
       p2 = _antisymmetric_smooth_potential(n, seed + 2000*i + 17, float(0.5 + 0.15*i), nfreq)
       cands.append(p2)
    # binary filtered
    for i in range(4):
       keep = [max(2, n//512), max(2, n//256), max(2, n//64), max(2, n//16)][i]
       keep = min(n//2, max(1, int(keep)))
       amp = [0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0][i]
       p = _binary_antisymmetric_potential(n, seed+3000*i+29, keep, amp)
       cands.append(p)
    if len(cands) > max_candidates:
       cands = cands[:max_candidates]
    return cands
```
But using negative indexing? For n large, `n//512` might be 0; max. Good.

`_binary_antisymmetric_potential`: if keep = 0? ensure >=1.
```
def _binary_antisymmetric_potential(n, seed, keep, amplitude):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    s = rng.choice(np.array([-1.0,1.0]), size=n)
    s = s - s[::-1]
    s[s > 0] = 1.0
    s[s < 0] = -1.0
    s[s == 0] = 0.0
    F = np.fft.rfft(s)
    K = min(F.size, max(1, int(keep)))
    F2 = F.copy(); F2[K:] = 0.0
    u = np.fft.irfft(F2, n)
    maxabs = float(np.max(np.abs(u))) if u.size else 0.0
    if maxabs > 1e-300:
        u /= maxabs
    else:
        u = np.zeros(n)
    p = 1.0 + amplitude * u
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-15)
    return _normalize_p(p)
```
For n odd, center s 0; mean zero. Low-pass includes DC maybe zero. If amplitudes produce p below 0, floor.

`_antisymmetric_smooth_potential`:
```
def _antisymmetric_smooth_potential(n, seed, amplitude, n_freq):
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    x=np.arange(n)/float(n)
    u=np.zeros(n)
    maxamp=0.0
    for m in range(1,n_freq+1):
       amp = np.sqrt(1.0/float(m)) * rng.uniform(0.3,1.0)
       if rng.random()<0.5: amp=-amp
       u += amp*np.sin(_two_pi*m*x)
    maxabs = ...; if >0: u/=maxabs
    p=1+amplitude*u; p=max(p,1e-15); normalize
```

`_random_smooth_potential`:
```
def _random_smooth_potential(n, seed, kappa, n_freq):
   rng=default_rng(seed)
   x=np.arange(n)/float(n)
   phi=zeros
   for m in range(1,n_freq+1):
      amp = np.sqrt(1.0/m)*rng.uniform(0.2,1.0)
      phase=rng.uniform(0,_two_pi)
      phi += amp*np.sin(_two_pi*m*x + phase)
   # subtract mean to avoid global exp scale
   phi -= float(np.mean(phi))
   p=np.exp(kappa*phi)
   p=np.maximum(p,1e-15)
   return _normalize_p(p)
```
Could include cosine by random phase; okay.

```
def _score_candidates(cands):
    scored=[]
    for p in cands:
       if p is None: continue
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)): continue
       score=_score_array(p)
       if np.isfinite(score): scored.append((score,p))
    scored.sort(key=_score_key)
    return scored
```

`_add_to_pool` top-level:
```
def _add_to_pool(pool, seq, custom, exact=None, max_size=80):
    arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if arr.size == 0 or float(arr.sum()) <= 0: return
    # normalize? no, score invariant. But to reduce memory maybe not.
    if len(pool) >= max_size:
        worst_idx=0; worst=pool[0][0]
        for i in range(1,len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] > worst:
                worst_idx=i; worst=pool[i][0]
        if custom >= worst:
            return
        del pool[worst_idx]
    pool.append((float(custom), float(exact) if exact is not None else np.inf, arr))
```
Use exact None -> inf. If exact finite, maybe custom less? Keep.

`_pool_final_key`:
```
def _pool_final_key(item):
    custom = item[0]
    exact = item[1]
    if exact is not None and np.isfinite(exact):
        return float(exact)
    return float(custom)
```

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    absolute_deadline = start + max(0.0, budget) - 1.0
    reserve = min(60.0, 0.05*budget)
    search_deadline = min(absolute_deadline, start + max(0.0, budget - reserve))
    total_budget = budget
    pool=[]
    best_parent = None
    best_custom = np.inf
    # Initialize parent from height_sequence_1 if any
    hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if hs is not None:
       arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
       arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0); arr = np.clip(arr,0,1000)
       if arr.size>0 and arr.sum()>0:
          arr = _normalize_p(arr)
          sc = _score_array(arr)
          if np.isfinite(sc):
             best_parent=arr.copy(); best_custom=sc; _add_to_pool(pool, arr, sc, np.inf)
    # also add uniform? maybe later in phases
    phases = [(512,0.03),(1024,0.06),(2048,0.10),(4096,0.18),(8192,0.26),(16384,0.30)]
    # If budget small, adjust: sum fractions 0.93. For budget <300, maybe skip large.
    for n, frac in phases:
       if time.monotonic() >= search_deadline: break
       alloc = frac * total_budget
       if alloc < 20.0: continue
       phase_start = time.monotonic()
       phase_end = min(search_deadline, phase_start + alloc)
       if phase_end - phase_start < 10.0: break
       # generate candidates
       num_random = 4 if alloc > 300 else 3
       max_cands = 36 if n <= 2048 else 30
       cands = _make_initial_candidates(n, int(seed + n*13), best_parent, num_random, max_cands)
       scored = _score_candidates(cands)
       if not scored: continue
       # top_k
       if n >= 8192: top_k = 2 if alloc > 200 else 1
       elif alloc > 250: top_k = 3
       elif alloc > 100: top_k = 2
       else: top_k = 1
       top_k = min(top_k, len(scored))
       T = phase_end - phase_start
       phase_best_seq = scored[0][1]; phase_best_score = scored[0][0]
       for idx in range(top_k):
           cand_deadline = phase_start + T * (idx + 1) / float(top_k)
           # leave some for refine if more candidates? If idx last, cand_deadline = phase_end.
           if cand_deadline <= time.monotonic(): continue
           # If top_k>1, leave 15% of remaining phase after last candidate? The formula last=phase_end, no refine. We can if idx==top_k-1 and T>60: cand_deadline = phase_start + T*0.85
           if top_k > 1 and idx == top_k - 1:
               cand_deadline = phase_start + T * 0.80
           elif top_k == 1 and T > 60:
               cand_deadline = phase_start + T * 0.75
           best_p, best_s = _optimize_sequence(n, scored[idx][1], cand_deadline, int(seed + 10*n + idx*97), tag=f"n{n}c{idx}")
           if best_s < phase_best_score:
               phase_best_score = best_s; phase_best_seq = best_p
           _add_to_pool(pool, best_p, best_s, np.inf)
       # final refine within phase if time
       if time.monotonic() < phase_end - 10 and phase_end - phase_start > 40:
           refine_p, refine_s = _optimize_sequence(n, phase_best_seq, phase_end, int(seed + 20*n + 5), tag=f"n{n}refine")
           if refine_s < phase_best_score:
               phase_best_score=refine_s; phase_best_seq=refine_p
           _add_to_pool(pool, refine_p, refine_s, np.inf)
       if phase_best_score < best_custom:
           best_custom = phase_best_score; best_parent = phase_best_seq.copy()
           print(f"Phase n={n} best {best_custom:.6f} time {time.monotonic()-start:.1f}")
       else:
           print(f"Phase n={n} no phase improvement, best parent {best_custom:.6f}")
       # Optional exact evaluate phase best if not too many? maybe skip.
```

Now, let's write optimizer code exactly.

```
def _optimize_sequence(n, initial_p, deadline, seed, tag=""):
    if initial_p is None or n <= 1:
       p = np.full(n, 1.0/max(1,n)); return p, 2.0 if n>0 else np.inf
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    p = _normalize_p(np.asarray(initial_p, dtype=np.float64))
    q = _fast_self_conv(p)
    score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(q)) if q.size else np.inf
    best = p.copy(); best_q = q.copy(); best_score = score
    last_print = best_score
    it = 0
    eta = 0.8
    min_eta = 0.05
    max_eta = 3.5
    base_eta = 0.8
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        p = np.full(n,1.0/n); q=_fast_self_conv(p); score=...
    print(f"{tag} init {best_score:.6f}")
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        # Check current score vs best
        if score < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = score
            best = p.copy()
            best_q = q.copy()
            if best_score < last_print - 1e-3:
                print(f"{tag} iter {it} score {best_score:.6f}")
                last_print = best_score
        # stall reset
        if it - best_it > 350:  # need best_it init 0? maintain
```
Need maintain `best_it`. Initially 0. In update best, set best_it=it. But at top before update, if improve set. Use `stall = it - best_it`. If best_it 0, after 350 no best.
```
        if it - best_it > 350 and score >= best_score:
            alpha = 0.1 + 0.2 * min(1.0, float(it - best_it)/500.0)
            noise = _random_smooth_potential(n, rng, 0.6, min(16, max(4, n//256)))
            p = _normalize_p((1.0-alpha)*best + alpha*noise)
            q = _fast_self_conv(p)
            score = float(2.0*n*np.max(q))
            if score < best_score - 1e-14:
                 update best, best_it=it
            eta = max(0.3, eta*0.6)
            best_it = it  # reset? If we set, avoid repeated perturb. But if still stuck, after 350 again. Good.
            continue
```
But setting best_it=it even if no improvement means it resets; if scoring not improving, it will perturb every 350. Good.

Now, weights. `maxq = float(np.max(q))`. If maxq <=0 break.
```
        tau_frac = max(0.02, 0.8 * math.exp(-0.0025 * it))
        tau = max(maxq * tau_frac, 1e-12)
        z = (q - maxq) / tau
        z = np.clip(z, -35.0, 0.0)
        s = np.exp(z)
        sm = float(s.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(sm) or sm <= 0:
            qs = float(q.sum())
            s = q / qs if qs>0 else np.full(q.size, 1.0/q.size)
        else:
            s = s / sm
        cross = _fast_guide(s, p)
        if cross.size != n:
           p=best; q=best_q; score=best_score; continue
        mean_cross = float(np.dot(p, cross))
        delta = cross - mean_cross
        max_delta = float(np.max(delta))
        if not np.isfinite(max_delta) or max_delta <= 1e-15:
           # small noise
           p = _normalize_p(p + 0.01*rng.standard_normal(n))
           q = _fast_self_conv(p); score=...
           if score < best_score: update
           eta = max(min_eta, eta*0.7)
           continue
        step = eta * delta / max_delta
        step = np.clip(step, -40.0, 40.0)
        p_new = p * np.exp(-step)
        p_new = _normalize_p(p_new)
        prev_score = score
        q = _fast_self_conv(p_new)
        score = float(2.0*n*np.max(q))
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            p = best.copy(); q = best_q.copy(); score = best_score; eta = max(min_eta, eta*0.5)
            continue
        p = p_new
        if score < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score=score; best=p.copy(); best_q=q.copy(); best_it=it
            if best_score < last_print - 1e-3: print...
        # adapt eta relative to previous
        if score < prev_score - 1e-14:
            eta = min(max_eta, eta * 1.05)
        else:
            eta = max(min_eta, eta * 0.85)
```
After loop, final update if score < best. Return best,best_score.

In `_normalize_p`, if p is scalar? assume array. If p has inf, replace. Use `p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)`? If input 2D? Candidates 1D. `reshape(-1)` ensures 1D but may change length. Use `.copy()`.
```
def _normalize_p(p, mix=0.0):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = p.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p[p < 0.0] = 0.0
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-18)
    total = float(p.sum())
    n = p.size
    if total <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0/float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    p = p / total
    if mix > 0.0:
        p = (1.0 - mix) * p + (mix / float(n))
        total = float(p.sum())
        if total > 0: p /= total
    return p
```
The floor 1e-18 may make a sequence with a coordinate intended zero become 1e-18; normalized sum still. For n large, total floor negligible. But if p is uniform, floor changes slightly; okay.

But if best_seq from exact known with value finite, best_val set. If no exact known but exact evaluations happened, best_val finite. If all evals skipped and best_seq fallback, best_val inf. OK.

Use `_pool_final_key` for sort, which uses exact if known (only few). Since most exact inf, sorts by custom.

Potential: If exact known much better but custom worse, it will sort top due exact. Good.

Now, if `best_seq` has dtype object? no.

Final clip:
```
arr = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
s = float(arr.sum())
if s > 0 and s < 1e6: arr = arr / s
elif s <=0: arr = np.full(arr.size, 1.0/max(arr.size,1))
return [float(v) for v in arr.tolist()]
```
If arr size huge, list to Python floats okay. 16384 length fine. If size >100000? We cap n=16384. Good.

Now, let's consider if the final exact sequence from pool has sum not 1 because maybe pool stored unnormalized? All candidates normalized; parent normalized. But final normalize anyway.

Now, need ensure `np.argsort` not used with lambda. Use `.sort`.

Let's produce final code.

Double-check top-level functions no references to undefined: `_first_key`, `_pool_final_key`, `_evaluate_array`, `_score_key`? Use one `_first_key`.

In `_score_candidates`, `scored.sort(key=_first_key)`.

In `propose_candidate`, when updating best_parent, if `phase_best_seq` maybe a view? We copy.

In `_optimize_sequence`, return `best` array; maybe contains floor >0. Good.

One subtle bug: In `_optimize_sequence`, at top after time loop, if `score` was updated at previous end and is new best, top updates best and prints. But `best_it` set? In top update, set `best_it = it`. In end update, set `best_it = it`. Good.

But if initial best_score at start, best_it=0. If after first update score worsens, best_it remains0 until 350; then perturb. Fine.

Pseudo final optimizer code:
```
    best_it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        if score < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = score; best = p.copy(); best_q = q.copy(); best_it = it; print...
        if it - best_it > 350:
            ... perturb
            if score < best_score - 1e-14: update best_it=it
            best_it = it
            continue
        ...
        if not finite revert ... continue
        p = p_new
        if score < best_score - 1e-14: update
        ...
    if score < best_score -1e-14: update
    return best, best_score
```
In perturb, after computing new p and q/score, if new score is new best, update best and best_it, then continue. If not, still set best_it=it to reset. This means if perturb new current is worse, best_it reset, so it won't perturb again until 350 iterations from perturb. Good.

In max_delta no signal, small noise and reset? We didn't reset best_it there. Could cause repeated zero noise if stuck. Add `best_it = it`? But if no signal, likely score flat; perturb maybe better. In that branch, after noise, if not improve, set `best_it=it` to avoid infinite? Let's do:
```
if max_delta invalid:
   p = _normalize_p(p * (1+0.02*normal))
   ...
   if not improve: best_it = it
   continue
```
But if it is improving slowly but no signal? no signal means cross uniform; objective likely stationary; perturbing okay.

In max_delta, if cross differences tiny due near-optimal, adding noise may hurt; but then score will worsen, eta adapt down; okay.

Now, maybe `np.dot(p, cross)` with p containing floor 1e-18, cross length n. good.

Potential bug: `np.max(delta)` if delta contains nan; use `np.nan_to_num(delta, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)` before max. Do after cross nan_to_num.

```
cross = np.nan_to_num(cross, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
delta = cross - mean_cross
delta = np.nan_to_num(delta, ...)
```

Now, if p contains all floor due to underflow, total huge? `_normalize_p` prevents.

Let's write final. Ensure no line too long? fine.

One more concern: The objective exact evaluator uses full convolution of sequence values; if we return normalized p sum1, and n large, max q ~1/n ~6e-5. 2*n*max ~1.5. Floating max from exact `np.convolve` of 16384 floats with direct O(n^2) may accumulate error maybe 1e-12, score error 1e-8. Fine.

Now, final output.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for low-score nonnegative sequences minimizing
    2 * n * max(conv(a, a)) / (sum(a)^2)

Strategy:
- Work with normalized probabilities p (sum=1), so score = 2n * max(p*p full conv).
- Fast evaluation and gradients using FFT-based convolution/correlation.
- Natural-gradient / multiplicative-weats updates driven by smooth-max peaks.
- Many deterministic/stochastic initializations, including antisymmetric Fourier
  and smoothed antisymmetric signed patterns, then coarse-to-fine length search.
- Keep a pool of strong custom-scored candidates and finish with exact
  evaluate_sequence() on the best ones.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

_TWO_PI = 2.0 * math.pi


def _next_power_of_two(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    m = int(m)
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize_p(p, mix=0.0):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    if p.ndim != 1:
        p = p.reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = p.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    p[p < 0.0] = 0.0
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-18)
    total = float(p.sum())
    n = p.size
    if total <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    p = p / total
    if mix > 0.0:
        p = (1.0 - float(mix)) * p + (float(mix) / float(n))
        total = float(p.sum())
        if total > 1e-300:
            p = p / total
    return p


def _fast_self_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.reshape(-1)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    target = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_power_of_two(target)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    q = q[:target]
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q), q, 0.0)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    return q


def _fast_guide(s, p):
    # cross guide_i = sum_k s_k p_{k-i}, valid i=0..n-1.
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    s = np.asarray(s, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if s.size < n:
        s = np.concatenate([s, np.zeros(n - s.size, dtype=np.float64)])
    target = s.size + n - 1
    N = _next_power_of_two(target)
    fs = np.fft.rfft(s, N)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fs * fp, N)
    g = c[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    return g


def _score_array(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.reshape(-1)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    total = float(a.sum())
    if total <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    p = a / total
    n = p.size
    q = _fast_self_conv(p)
    if q.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    maxq = float(np.max(q))
    if not np.isfinite(maxq):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * maxq)


def _first_key(item):
    return item[0]


def _pool_final_key(item):
    custom = item[0]
    exact = item[1]
    if exact is not None and np.isfinite(exact):
        return float(exact)
    return float(custom)


def _add_to_pool(pool, seq, custom, exact=None, max_size=90):
    arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.reshape(-1)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 1e-300:
        return
    if len(pool) >= max_size:
        worst_idx = 0
        worst_val = pool[0][0]
        for i in range(1, len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] > worst_val:
                worst_idx = i
                worst_val = pool[i][0]
        if float(custom) >= worst_val:
            return
        del pool[worst_idx]
    exact_val = float(exact) if exact is not None else np.inf
    pool.append((float(custom), exact_val, arr))


def _upsample(parent, n):
    parent = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    parent = np.where(np.isfinite(parent), parent, 0.0)
    parent = np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1000.0)
    m = parent.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if m == n:
        return _normalize_p(parent)
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    xs = (np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(m)
    xg = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    vals = np.interp(xg, xs, parent)
    vals = np.where(np.isfinite(vals), vals, 0.0)
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 0.0)
    return _normalize_p(vals)


def _random_smooth_potential(n, seed, kappa, n_freq):
    """Smooth random density proportional to exp(kappa * zero-mean Fourier field)."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    phi = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, int(n_freq) + 1):
        if rng.random() < 0.85:
            amp = math.sqrt(1.0 / float(m))
            amp *= float(rng.uniform(0.25, 1.0))
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, _TWO_PI))
            phi += amp * np.sin(_TWO_PI * float(m) * x + phase)
    phi -= float(np.mean(phi))
    p = np.exp(float(kappa) * phi)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-15)
    return _normalize_p(p)


def _antisymmetric_smooth_potential(n, seed, amplitude, n_freq):
    """
    Antisymmetric potential built from sine modes:
    u(1-x) = -u(x).  This directly reduces the central convolution coefficient.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    u = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, int(n_freq) + 1):
        amp = math.sqrt(1.0 / float(m)) * float(rng.uniform(0.30, 1.0))
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            amp = -amp
        u += amp * np.sin(_TWO_PI * float(m) * x)
    maxabs = float(np.max(np.abs(u))) if u.size else 0.0
    if maxabs > 1e-300:
        u = u / maxabs
    else:
        u = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p = 1.0 + float(amplitude) * u
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-15)
    return _normalize_p(p)


def _binary_antisymmetric_potential(n, seed, keep, amplitude):
    """Random ±1 antisymmetric sequence, Fourier low-passed, then mapped to 1+amplitude*u."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    s = rng.choice(np.array([-1.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64), size=n)
    s = s - s[::-1]
    s[s > 0.0] = 1.0
    s[s < 0.0] = -1.0
    s[s == 0.0] = 0.0
    F = np.fft.rfft(s)
    K = min(int(F.size), max(1, int(keep)))
    F2 = F.astype(np.complex128, copy=True)
    F2[K:] = 0.0
    u = np.fft.irfft(F2, n)
    maxabs = float(np.max(np.abs(u))) if u.size else 0.0
    if maxabs > 1e-300:
        u = u / maxabs
    else:
        u = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p = 1.0 + float(amplitude) * u
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-15)
    return _normalize_p(p)


def _make_initial_candidates(n, seed, parent, num_random, max_candidates):
    cands = []
    if n <= 0:
        return cands

    if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
        cands.append(_upsample(parent, n))

    cands.append(np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)

    if n <= 1400:
        for a in (-3.0, -2.0, -1.5, -1.0, -0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
            cands.append(_normalize_p(np.exp(float(a) * x)))

        for m in (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8):
            for A in (0.5, 0.8, 1.0):
                for phase in (0.0, 0.5 * math.pi, math.pi, 1.5 * math.pi):
                    p = 1.0 + float(A) * np.sin(_TWO_PI * float(m) * x + float(phase))
                    cands.append(_normalize_p(np.maximum(p, 1e-15)))

        for beta in (0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 1.0, 2.0):
            p = 1.0 / (x + float(beta))
            cands.append(_normalize_p(p))

    else:
        for a in (-2.0, -1.0, 1.0, 2.0):
            cands.append(_normalize_p(np.exp(float(a) * x)))

        for m in (1, 2, 3, 4):
            for A in (0.8, 1.0):
                for phase in (0.5 * math.pi, 1.5 * math.pi):
                    p = 1.0 + float(A) * np.sin(_TWO_PI * float(m) * x + float(phase))
                    cands.append(_normalize_p(np.maximum(p, 1e-15)))

    nfreq = int(min(32, max(4, n // 256)))

    for i in range(int(num_random)):
        kappa = (0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0)[i % 5]
        cands.append(_random_smooth_potential(n, int(seed) + 1000 * i + 7, kappa, nfreq))
        amp = float(0.5 + 0.15 * i)
        cands.append(_antisymmetric_smooth_potential(n, int(seed) + 2000 * i + 17, amp, nfreq))

    keep_choices = (
        max(2, n // 512),
        max(2, n // 256),
        max(2, n // 64),
        max(2, n // 16),
    )
    amps = (0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 1.0)
    for i in range(4):
        keep = int(min(n // 2, max(1, keep_choices[i])))
        cands.append(_binary_antisymmetric_potential(n, int(seed) + 3000 * i + 29, keep, amps[i]))

    if len(cands) > int(max_candidates):
        cands = cands[:int(max_candidates)]
    return cands


def _score_candidates(cands):
    scored = []
    for p in cands:
        if p is None:
            continue
        p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        if p.size == 0:
            continue
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            continue
        sc = _score_array(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((float(sc), p))
    scored.sort(key=_first_key)
    return scored


def _evaluate_array(arr):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        seq = [float(v) for v in a.tolist()]
        if not seq:
            return np.inf
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            return np.inf
        return val
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def _optimize_sequence(n, initial_p, deadline, seed, tag=""):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64), np.inf
    if deadline is None or time.monotonic() >= float(deadline):
        p = _normalize_p(np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n)))
        return p, float(_score_array(p))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    p = _normalize_p(np.asarray(initial_p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1))
    q = _fast_self_conv(p)
    score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(q)) if q.size else np.inf

    best = p.copy()
    best_q = q.copy()
    best_score = score
    best_it = 0
    last_print_score = best_score

    it = 0
    eta = 0.8
    min_eta = 0.05
    max_eta = 3.5

    print(f"{tag} start score={best_score:.6f} n={n}")

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        if score < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = float(score)
            best = p.copy()
            best_q = q.copy()
            best_it = it
            if best_score < last_print_score - 1e-3:
                print(f"{tag} iter={it} score={best_score:.6f}")
                last_print_score = best_score

        # Periodic escape: perturb from the current best if stuck.
        if it - best_it > 350 and score >= best_score - 1e-14:
            alpha = 0.1 + 0.2 * min(1.0, float(it - best_it) / 500.0)
            nfreq = int(min(16, max(4, n // 256)))
            noise = _random_smooth_potential(n, rng, 0.6, nfreq)
            p = _normalize_p((1.0 - alpha) * best + alpha * noise)
            q = _fast_self_conv(p)
            score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(q)) if q.size else np.inf
            if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score - 1e-14:
                best_score = float(score)
                best = p.copy()
                best_q = q.copy()
            best_it = it
            eta = max(0.3, eta * 0.6)
            continue

        maxq = float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0
        if maxq <= 0.0:
            break

        # Smooth-max weights over coefficients.
        tau_frac = max(0.02, 0.8 * math.exp(-0.0025 * float(it)))
        tau = max(maxq * tau_frac, 1e-12)
        z = (q - maxq) / tau
        z = np.clip(z, -35.0, 0.0)
        s = np.exp(z)
        sm = float(s.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(sm) or sm <= 0.0:
            qs = float(np.sum(q))
            if qs > 0.0:
                s = q / qs
            else:
                s = np.full(q.size, 1.0 / float(max(1, q.size)), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            s = s / sm

        cross = _fast_guide(s, p)
        if cross.size != n:
            raise RuntimeError("guide length mismatch")
        cross = np.nan_to_num(cross, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

        mean_cross = float(np.dot(p, cross))
        delta = cross - mean_cross
        delta = np.nan_to_num(delta, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        max_delta = float(np.max(delta))

        if not np.isfinite(max_delta) or max_delta <= 1e-15:
            p = _normalize_p(p * (1.0 + 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n)))
            q = _fast_self_conv(p)
            score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(q)) if q.size else np.inf
            if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score - 1e-14:
                best_score = float(score)
                best = p.copy()
                best_q = q.copy()
                best_it = it
            else:
                best_it = it
            eta = max(min_eta, eta * 0.7)
            continue

        step = float(eta) * delta / max_delta
        step = np.clip(step, -40.0, 40.0)
        p_new = p * np.exp(-step)
        p_new = _normalize_p(p_new)

        prev_score = score
        q = _fast_self_conv(p_new)
        score = float(2.0 * n * np.max(q)) if q.size else np.inf

        if not np.isfinite(score):
            p = best.copy()
            q = best_q.copy()
            score = best_score
            eta = max(min_eta, eta * 0.5)
            continue

        p = p_new

        if score < best_score - 1e-14:
            best_score = float(score)
            best = p.copy()
            best_q = q.copy()
            best_it = it
            if best_score < last_print_score - 1e-3:
                print(f"{tag} iter={it} score={best_score:.6f}")
                last_print_score = best_score

        if score < prev_score - 1e-14:
            eta = min(max_eta, eta * 1.05)
        else:
            eta = max(min_eta, eta * 0.85)

    if score < best_score - 1e-14:
        best = p.copy()
        best_score = float(score)

    print(f"{tag} done score={best_score:.6f} iters={it}")
    return best, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    absolute_deadline = start + budget - 1.0

    reserve = min(60.0, 0.05 * budget)
    search_deadline = max(start + 1.0, min(absolute_deadline, start + budget - reserve))
    search_budget = max(1.0, search_deadline - start)

    pool = []
    best_parent = None
    best_custom = np.inf

    # Optional parent from the supplied previous global, if available.
    hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if hs is not None:
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
            arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if arr.size > 0 and float(arr.sum()) > 1e-300:
                arr = _normalize_p(arr)
                sc = _score_array(arr)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    best_parent = arr.copy()
                    best_custom = float(sc)
                    _add_to_pool(pool, arr, sc, np.inf)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Phase fractions sum to 1.0.  Length grows, giving more search to larger n.
    phases = (
        (256, 0.015),
        (512, 0.030),
        (1024, 0.060),
        (2048, 0.100),
        (4096, 0.180),
        (8192, 0.280),
        (16384, 0.335),
    )

    for n, frac in phases:
        if time.monotonic() >= search_deadline:
            break
        alloc = frac * search_budget
        if alloc < 15.0:
            continue

        phase_start = time.monotonic()
        phase_end = min(search_deadline, phase_start + alloc)
        T = phase_end - phase_start
        if T <= 5.0:
            break

        max_cands = 36 if n <= 2048 else 28
        num_random = 4 if alloc > 300 else 3
        cands = _make_initial_candidates(
            int(n),
            int(seed) + int(n) * 13,
            best_parent,
            num_random,
            max_cands,
        )
        scored = _score_candidates(cands)
        if not scored:
            continue

        if n >= 8192:
            top_k = 2 if alloc > 200 else 1
        elif T > 250:
            top_k = 3
        elif T > 100:
            top_k = 2
        else:
            top_k = 1
        top_k = min(top_k, len(scored))

        phase_best_seq = scored[0][1]
        phase_best_score = scored[0][0]

        for idx in range(top_k):
            cand_deadline = phase_start + T * (idx + 1) / float(top_k)
            # Leave a refine slot at the end of the phase when meaningful.
            if idx == top_k - 1 and T > 80.0:
                cand_deadline = phase_start + 0.80 * T
            if cand_deadline <= time.monotonic():
                continue

            bp, bs = _optimize_sequence(
                int(n),
                scored[idx][1],
                cand_deadline,
                int(seed) + 10 * int(n) + idx * 97,
                tag=f"n{n}c{idx}",
            )
            if np.isfinite(bs):
                _add_to_pool(pool, bp, bs, np.inf)
                if bs < phase_best_score:
                    phase_best_score = float(bs)
                    phase_best_seq = bp

        if T > 60.0 and time.monotonic() < phase_end - 10.0:
            rp, rs = _optimize_sequence(
                int(n),
                phase_best_seq,
                phase_end,
                int(seed) + 20 * int(n) + 5,
                tag=f"n{n}ref",
            )
            if np.isfinite(rs):
                _add_to_pool(pool, rp, rs, np.inf)
                if rs < phase_best_score:
                    phase_best_score = float(rs)
                    phase_best_seq = rp

        if phase_best_score < best_custom:
            best_custom = float(phase_best_score)
            best_parent = np.asarray(phase_best_seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            print(f"Phase n={n}: best custom={best_custom:.6f} elapsed={time.monotonic() - start:.1f}")
        else:
            print(f"Phase n={n}: no phase improvement; global custom={best_custom:.6f}")

    # If time remains, do one final larger-length polish from the best parent.
    remaining = search_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 45.0 and best_parent is not None and time.monotonic() < search_deadline:
        n_extra = 16384 if best_parent.size >= 8192 else 8192
        cands = _make_initial_candidates(int(n_extra), int(seed) + 99991, best_parent, 3, 14)
        scored = _score_candidates(cands)
        if scored:
            bp, bs = _optimize_sequence(
                int(n_extra),
                scored[0][1],
                search_deadline,
                int(seed) + 99997,
                tag="extra_large",
            )
            if np.isfinite(bs):
                _add_to_pool(pool, bp, bs, np.inf)
                if bs < best_custom:
                    best_custom = float(bs)
                    best_parent = np.asarray(bp, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                    print(f"extra_large best custom={best_custom:.6f}")

    # Final exact selection.
    if pool:
        pool.sort(key=_pool_final_key)
        fallback_seq = pool[0][2]
    elif best_parent is not None:
        fallback_seq = best_parent
    else:
        fallback_seq = np.full(1024, 1.0 / 1024.0, dtype=np.float64)
        _add_to_pool(pool, fallback_seq, _score_array(fallback_seq), np.inf)

    best_val = np.inf
    best_seq = None

    if pool:
        total_evals = 0
        large_evals = 0
        max_total_evals = 30
        max_large_evals = 8

        # Re-sort after possible additions, now using exact values when available.
        pool.sort(key=_pool_final_key)

        for item in pool:
            if time.monotonic() > absolute_deadline - 3.0:
                break
            custom, exact, seq = item
            if exact is not None and np.isfinite(exact):
                val = float(exact)
            else:
                L = int(np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).size)
                if L > 10000 and large_evals >= max_large_evals:
                    continue
                if total_evals >= max_total_evals:
                    break
                val = _evaluate_array(seq)
                total_evals += 1
                if L > 10000:
                    large_evals += 1

            if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_val:
                best_val = float(val)
                best_seq = seq

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = fallback_seq

    # One last exact probe on the fallback if we did not land on it.
    if time.monotonic() < absolute_deadline - 2.0:
        probe = _evaluate_array(best_seq)
        if np.isfinite(probe):
            best_val = probe

    arr = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.full(1024, 1.0 / 1024.0, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s > 1e-300:
        arr = arr / s
    else:
        arr = np.full(arr.size, 1.0 / float(max(1, arr.size)), dtype=np.float64)

    print(f"final selected exact/approx score={best_val:.6f} len={arr.size}")
    return [float(v) for v in arr.tolist()]
```
